\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}(\overline{h}(\psi_{i-1})-h_{i}(\psi_{i-1}))\right\rVert^{2}\right]}\,\leq\,2\sqrt{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal I({\psi}^{\star})^{-1})}{n}$}}}\\
+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\cdnorm*{\mathcal I(\psi^\star)^{-2}}^{1/2}_{\mathrm F}}{n}$}}\left((M+\alpha^{m}C_{\chi}(\bar{\tau}^{1/2}+2\|\log Z\|_{4,\infty}\sigma))^{1/2}\left(\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{i-1}^{1/2}\right)^{1/2}\right.\\
+\left.\alpha^{2m}C_{\chi}^{2}\sigma^{2}\left(\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{i-1}\right)^{1/2}\right)\\

(18)

where

M\coloneqq\sup_{\psi\in\Psi}\left\lVert\nabla_{\psi}^{3}\log Z(\psi)\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}(\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{F},\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{F})}<+\infty
.

Proof.

Let us first note that

\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}(\overline{h}(\psi_{n-1})-h_{n}(\psi_{n-1}))

\displaystyle=f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}\underbrace{(\phi(X_{n})-\mathbb{E}\left[\phi(X_{1})\right])}\limits_{\Delta_{1,n}}+{f}^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}\underbrace{\left(\phi(K^{m}_{\psi_{n-1}}(X_{n}))-\mathbb{E}\left[\phi(K^{m}_{\psi_{n-1}}(X_{n}))\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{n-1}\right]\right)}_{\Delta_{2,n}}

\displaystyle=f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}\Delta_{1,n}+f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}\Delta_{2,n}\,.

Noting
\Delta
the l.h.s of Lemma
D.9
, we have, summing over
[n]
, and using Minkowski’s inequality,

\Delta\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}\Delta_{1,i}\right\rVert^{2}\right]}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}\Delta_{2,i}\right\rVert^{2}\right]}

Note that this step was made possible because we are looking at the square-root of the variance,
which is unlike the recursion in Lemma
3.1
. This allows to separate the terms and
use fewer intermediaries than in the proof of Lemma
3.1
.

Since both
\Delta_{1,n}
and
\Delta_{2,n}
are martingale differences with respect to the
filtration
\mathcal{F}_{n-1}
, the covariance terms vanish, and we have

\displaystyle\Delta

\displaystyle\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\sqrt{\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}\Delta_{1,i}\right\rVert^{2}\right]}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\sqrt{\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}\Delta_{2,i}\right\rVert^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\sqrt{\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{tr}(f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{1,i}\Delta_{1,i}^{\top}f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1})\right]}

\displaystyle\quad+\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\sqrt{\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{tr}(f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta_{2,i}\Delta_{2,i}^{\top}f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1})\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal I({\psi}^{\star})^{-1})}{n}$}}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\sqrt{\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{tr}(f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Cov}\left[\phi(K^{m}_{\psi_{i-1}}(X_{i}))\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]\right]f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1})}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal I({\psi}^{\star})^{-1})}{{n}}$}}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\Big(\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{tr}(f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{Cov}\left[\phi(X_{i})\right]+\left(\mathrm{Cov}\left[\phi(X^{\psi_{i-1}}_{i})\,\lvert\,\psi_{i-1}\right]\right.\right.\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\left.\left.-\mathrm{Cov}\left[\phi(X_{i})\right]\right)+\left(\mathrm{Cov}\left[\phi(K^{m}_{\psi_{i-1}}(X_{i}))\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]-\mathrm{Cov}\left[\phi(X^{\psi_{i-1}})\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]\right)\right]\right)f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1})\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal I({\psi}^{\star})^{-1})}{n}$}}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal I(\psi^\star)^{-2})}}{n}$}}\Big(\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\mathrm{Cov}\left[\phi(X^{\psi_{i-1}})\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right]-\mathrm{Cov}\left[\phi(X_{i})\right]\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\mathrm{Cov}\left[\phi(K^{m}_{\psi}(X_{i}))\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\psi_{i-1}\right]-\mathrm{Cov}\left[\phi(X^{\psi_{i-1}})\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{i-1}\right])\|_{\mathrm{F}}\right]\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}2\sqrt{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal I({\psi}^{\star})^{-1})}{n}$}}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal I(\psi^\star)^{-2})}}{n}$}}\left((M+\alpha^{m}C_{\chi}(\bar{\tau}^{1/2}+2\|\log Z\|_{4,\infty}\sigma)^{1/2})\times\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.\sqrt{\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\psi_{i-1}-\psi^{\star}\right\rVert\right]}+\alpha^{m}C_{\chi}\sigma\sqrt{\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\psi_{i-1}-\psi^{\star}\right\rVert^{2}\right]}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}2\sqrt{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal I({\psi}^{\star})^{-1})}{n}$}}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal I(\psi^\star)^{-2})}}{n}$}}\left((M+\alpha^{m}C_{\chi}(\bar{\tau}^{1/2}+2\|\log Z\|_{4,\infty}\sigma))^{1/2}\sqrt{\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{i}^{1/2}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.+\alpha^{m}C_{\chi}\sigma\sqrt{\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{i}}\right)

In
(a)
we used Lemma
D.9
,
the cyclicity of the trace,
\mathrm{tr}(A^{\top}B)\leq\left\lVert AB\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\left\lVert B\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}
,
and the fact that since
\mathrm{Cov}\left[\phi(X^{\psi_{i-1}})\right]=\nabla_{\psi}^{2}\mathcal{L}(\psi_{i-1})
, by analycity of
\mathcal{L}
,
there exists a constant

M\coloneqq\sup_{\psi\in\Psi}\left\lVert\nabla^{3}\log Z(\psi)\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}(\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert,\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{F})}
such that

\left\lVert\nabla_{\psi}^{2}\mathcal{L}(\psi_{i-1})-\nabla_{\psi}^{2}\mathcal{L}({\psi}^{\star})\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\leq M\left\lVert\psi_{i-1}-{\psi}^{\star}\right\rVert.

In
(b)
we used Jensen’s inequality to get

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\psi_{i-1}-{\psi}^{\star}\right\rVert\right]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\psi_{i-1}-{\psi}^{\star}\right\rVert^{2}\right]}=\delta_{i-1}^{1/2}
.
∎

We are now ready to prove Theorem
3.3
.

Proof of Theorem
3.3
.

It holds that:

\displaystyle f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})(\psi_{n-1}-{\psi}^{\star})

\displaystyle=f^{\prime}(\psi_{n-1})-f^{\prime}({\psi}^{\star})+\left(f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})(\psi_{n-1}-{\psi}^{\star})-f^{\prime}(\psi_{n-1})+f^{\prime}({\psi}^{\star})\right)

\displaystyle=h_{n}(\psi_{n-1})-f^{\prime}({\psi}^{\star})+\left(f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})(\psi_{n-1}-{\psi}^{\star})-f^{\prime}(\psi_{n-1})+f^{\prime}({\psi}^{\star})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+(f^{\prime}(\psi_{n-1})-\bar{h}(\psi_{n-1}))+(\overline{h}(\psi_{n-1})-h_{n}(\psi_{n-1})).

Applying on both sides: (a) a summation over
i\in[n]
, (b) a multiplication by
f^{\prime\prime}(\psi^{\star})^{-1}
, (c)
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\|\cdot\|^{2}]}
, and using Minkowski’s inequality on the r.h.s, we obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\psi_{i}-{\psi}^{\star}\right\rVert^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}h_{i}(\psi_{i-1})\right\rVert^{2}\right]}}\limits_{(i)}

\displaystyle\hskip-100.00015pt+\underbrace{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}(f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})(\psi_{i-1}-{\psi}^{\star})-f^{\prime}(\psi_{i-1})\right\rVert^{2}\right]}}\limits_{(ii)}

\displaystyle\hskip-100.00015pt+\underbrace{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}(f^{\prime}(\psi_{i-1})-\bar{h}(\psi_{i-1}))\right\rVert^{2}\right]}}\limits_{(iii)}

\displaystyle\hskip-100.00015pt+\underbrace{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}f^{\prime\prime}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}(\overline{h}(\psi_{i-1})-h_{i}(\psi_{i-1}))\right\rVert^{2}\right]}}\limits_{(iv)}.

(i)
and
(ii)
have direct analogues in the proofs of prior work
[
22
]
on the convergence of (unbiased) SGD with Polyak-Ruppert averaging, and will be bounded similarly.
(iii)
captures the bias of the CD algorithm, while
(iv)
captures the variance.

Bounding
(i)

Using Lemma
D.6
, we have

(i)=\mathcal{O}\left(n^{\max\left(\frac{\beta}{2}-1,-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1}\right)}\right)
.

Bounding
(ii)

Since
\log Z(\psi)
is analytic, there exists some constant
M^{\prime}
such that

\left\lVert f^{\prime\prime}(\psi^{\star})(\psi_{i-1}-\psi^{\star})-f^{\prime}(\psi_{i-1})\right\rVert\leq M^{\prime}\left\lVert\psi_{i-1}-\psi^{\star}\right\rVert^{2}.

Thus, we have:

(ii)\leq\frac{M^{\prime}}{n}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\msum_{i=1}^{n}\|\psi_{i}-\psi^{\star}\|^{2}\right)^{2}\right]}\leq\frac{M^{\prime}}{n}\msum_{i=1}^{n}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\psi_{i}-\psi^{\star}\right\rVert^{4}\right]}=\mathcal{O}(n^{-\beta})

where the second-to-last inequality used Minkowski’s inequality, and the last applied Lemma
D.7
.

Bounding
(iii)

By Minkowski’s inequality, we have:

(iii)\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert f^{\prime}(\psi_{n-1})-\bar{h}(\psi_{n-1})\right\rVert^{2}\right]}

Moreover, using lemma
C.7
, we have:

\displaystyle\left\lVert f^{\prime}(\psi_{n-1})-\bar{h}(\psi_{n-1})\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\mathbb{E}\left[\phi(X^{\psi_{n-1}})\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{n-1}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{P}_{\psi_{n-1}}^{m}\phi(X_{n})\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{n-1}\right]\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{m}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\phi(X^{\psi_{n-1}})-\mathbb{E}\left[\phi(X^{\psi_{n-1}})\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{n-1}\right]\right\rVert^{2}\nonscript\>\middle|\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\mathcal{F}_{n-1}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}\left\lVert\psi_{n-1}-{\psi}^{\star}\right\rVert.

We thus obtain

(iii)\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\alpha^{m} C_{\chi}}{n}$}}\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\psi_{i-1}-{\psi}^{\star}\right\rVert\vphantom{a}^{2}\right]\right)^{1/2}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\alpha^{m} C_{\chi}}{n}$}}\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{i-1}^{1/2}.

Recalling that
\delta_{i}
satisfies Theorem
3.2
, we have that
\delta_{n}^{\frac{1}{2}}=\mathcal{O}(n^{-\beta/2})
, and we thus have
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{i}^{1/2}=\mathcal{O}(n^{1-\frac{\beta}{2}})
.
By squaring the result of Lemma
D.5
, we have
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{i}^{1/2}=\mathcal{O}(n^{-1-\frac{\beta}{2}})
, and thus,
we obtain that

(iii)=\mathcal{O}(\alpha^{m}n^{-\beta/2})=\mathcal{O}(n^{-(\frac{\beta}{2}+m\frac{\lvert\log\alpha\rvert}{\log n})})
.

Bounding
(iv)

We have

\displaystyle(iv)

\displaystyle\,\overset{(a)}{\leq}\,2\sqrt{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mathrm{tr} \cdpar*{\mathcal I({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}}}{n}$}}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\cdnorm*{\mathcal I(\psi^\star)^{-2}}^{1/2}_{\mathrm F}}{n}$}}\left((M+\alpha^{m}C_{\chi}(\bar{\tau}+2\|\log Z\|_{4,\infty}\sigma))^{1/2}\sqrt{\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{i-1}^{1/2}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.+\alpha^{m}C_{\chi}\sigma\sqrt{\msum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{i-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}2\sqrt{\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mathrm{tr} \cdpar*{\mathcal I({\psi}^{\star})^{-1}}}{n}$}}}+\mathcal{O}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta}{4}}).

Where in
(a)
, we used Lemma
D.9
and in
(b)
, we used Lemma
D.5
and
D.4
.

Final bound

Putting everything together, we have that:

\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert\overline{\psi}_{n}-{\psi}^{\star}\right\rVert^{2}\right]}\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{I}({\psi}^{\star})^{-1})}{n}}+\mathcal{O}\left(n^{\max\left(-\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta}{4}\right),-\beta,\frac{\beta}{2}-1,-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1},-\left(\frac{\beta}{2}+m\frac{\left\lvert\log\alpha\right\rvert}{\log n}\right)\right)}\right)\,.

If, furthermore,
m>\frac{(1-\beta)\log n}{2|\log\alpha|}
, we have

\max\left(-\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta}{4}\right),-\beta,\frac{\beta}{2}-1,-\frac{2\beta}{2\beta+1},-\left(\frac{\beta}{2}+m\frac{\left\lvert\log\alpha\right\rvert}{\log n}\right)\right)<-\frac{1}{2},

which concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix E
L_{2}
approximation by auxiliary gradient updates

In this section, we consider different gradient update schemes starting from some random initialization
\theta_{\rm init}
, and control the
L_{2}
distance between the different updates and the deterministic target
\psi^{*}\in\Psi
.

Notation.

Let
\theta^{\rm init}
be some
\Psi
-valued random initialization that is possibly correlated with
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
. We capture the effect of correlation through the following quantities: For
\epsilon>0
and
\nu>2
, let

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\rm init}_{n,m}(\epsilon)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;{\mathbbm{P}}\Bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \Big\| \sum_{i=1}^n \mean\big[ \phi\big(K^m_{\theta^{\rm init}}(X_i) \big) \,\big|\, X_i, \theta^{\rm init} \big]
-
\mean\big[ \phi\big(K^m_{\theta_{\rm init}}(X'_i)\big) \,\big|\, \theta^{\rm init}
\big] \Big\| }{n}$}}\,>\,\epsilon\Bigg)\;,

\displaystyle\text{and }\quad\varepsilon^{\rm init}_{n,m;\nu}(\epsilon)\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\sqrt{\epsilon^{2}+\kappa_{\nu;m}^{2}\big(\vartheta^{\rm init}_{n,m}(\epsilon)\big)^{\frac{2}{\nu-2}}}\;.

For notational clarity, we shall use
\theta_{m,B}
to denote parameters arising from an one-step update, where the subscripts
m,B
represent performing the one-step update with length-
m
Markov chains and with batch size
B
. This is to be distinguished from
\psi_{t}
elsewhere in the text, which denotes the parameter from the actual multi-step CD algorithm and the subscript
t
denotes the
t
-th CD iterate.

Gradient update schemes.

We consider five different updates. Let
X^{\prime}_{1}
be an i.i.d. copy of
X_{1}
drawn independently of all other random variables. The SGD-with-replacement update is given by

\displaystyle\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}\;\coloneqq\;F^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}(\theta^{\rm init})\;,\quad\text{ where }F^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}(\psi)\;\coloneqq\;\psi-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\eta}{B}$}}\msum_{i\in S^{w}}\Big(\phi(X_{i})-\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\psi}(X_{i})\big)\Big)

and
S^{w}
is a uniformly drawn size-
B
subset of
[n]
. The SGD-without-replacement update, after renormalizing the learning rate, is given by the
N
-fold function composition

\displaystyle\;\theta^{\rm SGDo}_{m,B}\;\coloneqq\;F^{\rm SGDo}_{m,B;N}\circ\ldots\circ F^{\rm SGDo}_{m,B;1}(\theta^{\rm init})\;,

\displaystyle\;\hskip 30.00005pt\text{ where }\quad F^{\rm SGDo}_{m,B;j}(\psi)\;\coloneqq\;\psi-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\eta}{NB}$}}\msum_{i\in S^{o}_{j}}\Big(\phi(X_{i})-\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\psi}(X_{i})\big)\Big)\;\;\text{ for each }j\in[N]\;,

and
S^{o}_{j}
’s are disjoint size-
B
random subsets of
[n]
, defined by
(S^{o}_{1},\ldots,S^{o}_{N})=\pi([n])
for a uniformly drawn element
\pi
of the permutation group on
n
objects. The full-batch gradient update is given by

\displaystyle\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}\;\coloneqq\;F^{\rm GD}_{m}(\theta^{\rm init})\;,\quad\text{ where }\quad F^{\rm GD}_{m}(\psi)\;\coloneqq\;\psi-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\eta}{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}\Big(\phi(X_{i})-\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\psi}(X_{i})\big)\Big)\;.

The full-batch gradient update with an infinite-length Markov chain is given by

\displaystyle\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}\;\coloneqq\;F^{\rm GD}_{\infty}(\theta^{\rm init})\;,\qquad\text{ where }\qquad F^{\rm GD}_{\infty}(\psi)\;\coloneqq\;\psi-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\eta}{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}\Big(\phi(X_{i})-\phi(X^{\psi}_{1})\Big)\;.

The population gradient update with an infinite-length Markov chain is given by

\displaystyle\theta^{\rm pop}\;\coloneqq\;f^{\rm pop}(\theta^{\rm init})\;,\qquad\text{ where }\quad f^{\rm pop}(\psi)\;\coloneqq\;\psi-\eta\,\mathbb{E}\big[\phi(X_{1})-\phi(X^{\psi}_{1})\big]\;,

where we use the lowercase
f
to emphasize that
f^{\rm pop}
is a deterministic function.

The forthcoming results are summarized below:

\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}\;\overset{\rm\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:SGDw:GD}}{\approx}\;\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}\;\overset{\rm\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:GD:sample:trunc:error}}{\approx}\;\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}\;\overset{\rm\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:GD:sample:pop:error}}{\approx}\;\theta^{\rm pop}\;\overset{\rm\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:GD:pop:opt:error}}{\approx}\;\psi^{*}\;.

Lemma E.1
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{n}
be the sigma algebra generated by
\{X_{i},K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{i})\,|\,1\leq i\leq n\}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}\,\big|\,\theta^{\rm init},\mathcal{F}_{n}\big]\;=

\displaystyle\;0\qquad\text{ almost surely}\;.

Moreover, under
A1
and
A7
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\|\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}\big\|^{2}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 \eta^2 ( \sigma^2 + \kappa_{\nu;m}^2 )}{B}$}}\,\mathbb{I}_{\{B<n\}}\;.

Proof of
Lemma

E.1
.

Write
A\coloneqq(A_{1},\ldots,A_{n})
, where

\displaystyle A_{i}\;\coloneqq\;\big(\phi(X_{i})-\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{i})\big)\big)-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi(X_{1})-\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{1})\big)\big]\;.

Since
S^{w}
is uniformly drawn from all size-
B
subsets of
[n]
and independently of all other variables, we have that almost surely

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}\,\big|\,\theta^{\rm init},\mathcal{F}_{n}\big]\;=\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\eta}{B}$}}\msum_{i\in S^{w}}A_{i}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\eta}{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}A_{i}\,\Big|\,A\Big]\;=\;0\;.

To prove the remaining bound, we note that the above relation implies
\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}
is zero-mean. By the law of total variance, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\|\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}\big\|^{2}\;=\;\text{\rm Tr}\,\text{\rm Cov}\big[\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}\big]\;=\;\text{\rm Tr}\,\mathbb{E}\,\text{\rm Cov}\big[\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}\,\big|\,\theta^{\rm init},\mathcal{F}_{n}\big]

\displaystyle\;=\;\eta^{2}\,\text{\rm Tr}\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 }{B}$}}\msum_{i\in S^{w}}A_{i}\Big)\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 }{B}$}}\msum_{i\in S^{w}}A_{i}\Big)^{\top}\,\Big|\,A\Big]-\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 }{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}A_{i}\Big)\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 }{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}A_{i}\Big)^{\top}\,\bigg]\;.

To compute the covariance, recall that
S^{w}
is a uniformly drawn size-
B
subset of
[n]
with
B=n/N
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{N}([n])
be the collection of all partitions of
[n]
into
N
size-
B
subsets. We can generate
S^{w}
by the following two-step process:

(i)

Uniformly draw a partition
P^{\prime}=(P^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,P^{\prime}_{N})
from
\mathcal{P}_{N}([n])
;

(ii)

Uniformly sample an index
K
from
[N]
and set
S^{w}=P^{\prime}_{K}
.

Then we have, almost surely

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 }{B}$}}\msum_{i\in S^{w}}A_{i}\Big)\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 }{B}$}}\msum_{i\in S^{w}}A_{i}\Big)^{\top}\,\Big|\,A\Big]-\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 }{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}A_{i}\Big)\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1 }{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}A_{i}\Big)^{\top}

\displaystyle\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{|\cP_N([n])|}$}}\msum_{P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{N}([n])}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{N}$}}\msum_{k\leq N}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{B^2}$}}\msum_{i,j\in P^{\prime}_{k}}A_{i}A_{j}^{\top}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n^2}$}}\msum_{i,j\leq n}A_{i}A_{j}^{\top}\,\Big)

\displaystyle\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{|\cP_N([n])|}$}}\msum_{P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{N}([n])}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{N B^2}$}}\msum_{k\leq N}\msum_{i,j\in P^{\prime}_{k}}A_{i}A_{j}^{\top}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{N^2 B^2}$}}\msum_{k,l\leq N}\msum_{i\in P^{\prime}_{k},j\in P^{\prime}_{l}}A_{i}A_{j}^{\top}\,\Big)

\displaystyle\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{|\cP_N([n])|}$}}\msum_{P^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{N}([n])}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{N-1}{N^2 B^2}$}}\msum_{k\leq N}\msum_{i,j\in P^{\prime}_{k}}A_{i}A_{j}^{\top}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{N^2 B^2}$}}\msum_{k\neq l}\msum_{i\in P^{\prime}_{k},j\in P^{\prime}_{l}}A_{i}A_{j}^{\top}\,\Big)\,.

By noting that
A_{i}
’s are exchangeable, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big\|\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}-

\displaystyle\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}\big\|^{2}\;=\;\eta^{2}\text{\rm Tr}\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{N-1}{N B}$}}A_{1}A_{1}^{\top}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(N-1)(B-1)}{N B}$}}A_{1}A_{2}^{\top}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{N-1}{N}$}}A_{1}A_{2}^{\top}\,\bigg]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\eta^{2}\text{\rm Tr}\,\mathbb{E}\bigg[\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{N-1}{N B}$}}A_{1}A_{1}^{\top}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{N-1}{N B}$}}A_{1}A_{2}^{\top}\,\bigg]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\eta^2 (N-1)}{NB}$}}\big(\mathbb{E}\|A_{1}\|^{2}-\mathbb{E}\langle A_{1},A_{2}\rangle\big)

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \eta^2}{B}$}}\mathbb{E}\|A_{1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \eta^2}{B}$}}\mathbb{E}\Big\|\big(\phi(X_{1})-\mathbb{E}[\phi(X_{1})]\big)-\Big(\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{1})\big)-\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{1})\big)\Big]\Big)\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 \eta^2}{B}$}}\Big(\text{\rm Tr}\,\text{\rm Cov}[\phi(X_{1})]+\text{\rm Tr}\,\text{\rm Cov}\Big[\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{1})\big)\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\;\overset{(b)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 \eta^2 ( \sigma^2 + \kappa_{\nu;m}^2 )}{B}$}}\;.

In
(a)
, we have used a Cauchy-Schwarz inequality; in
(b)
, we have used
A1
and
A7
. Finally we note that if
B=n
,
\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}=\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}
almost surely, which implies the desired bound.
∎

Lemma E.2
.

Denote
\mathcal{A}_{n}
as the sigma algebra generated by
\theta^{\rm init},X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
. Under
A1
,
A2
,
A3
and
A7
, we have that for any
\epsilon>0
and
\nu>2
,

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\,\big\|\mathbb{E}\big[\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}\,\big|\,\mathcal{A}_{n}\big]\big\|^{2}\;\leq\;\eta^{2}\Big(\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}\,\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{\rm init}-\psi^{*}\|^{2}}\,+\,\varepsilon^{\rm init}_{n,m;\nu}(\epsilon)\Big)^{2}\;,

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\,\|\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}\|^{2}\;\leq\;\eta^{2}\Big(\Big(\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}\,\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{\rm init}-\psi^{*}\|^{2}}\,+\,\varepsilon^{\rm init}_{n,m;\nu}(\epsilon)\Big)^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\kappa_{\nu;m}^2 + \sigma^2}{n}$}}\Big)\;.

Proof of
Lemma

E.2
.

The main challenge arises from the possible correlation between
\theta^{\rm init}
and
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
. First note that for any
\epsilon>0
,
\nu>2
and a real-valued random variable
Y
, by Hölder’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[Y^{2}]\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big[Y^{2}\,\mathbb{I}_{\{Y\leq\epsilon\}}+Y^{2}\,\mathbb{I}_{\{Y>\epsilon\}}\big]

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\epsilon^{2}+\mathbb{E}[Y^{2}\mathbb{I}_{\{Y>\epsilon\}}]\;\leq\;\epsilon^{2}+(\mathbb{E}[Y^{\nu}])^{2/\nu}{\mathbbm{P}}(Y>\epsilon)^{(\nu-2)/\nu}\;.

(19)

Also note the useful inequality that for two real-valued random vectors (possibly correlated)
V_{1},V_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|V_{1}+V_{2}\|^{2}]\;\leq\;\mathbb{E}[(\|V_{1}\|+\|V_{2}\|)^{2}]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\|V_{1}\|^{2}+2\sqrt{(\mathbb{E}\|V_{1}\|^{2})(\mathbb{E}\|V_{2}\|^{2})}+\mathbb{E}\|V_{2}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\big(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|V_{1}\|^{2}}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|V_{2}\|^{2}}\big)^{2}\;.

(20)

Now to control the first quantity of interest, by using a triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,\big\|\mathbb{E}\big[\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}\,\big|\,\mathcal{A}_{n}\big]\big\|^{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big\|\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\eta}{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}\big(\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{i})\big)-\phi\big(X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_{i}\big)\big)\,\Big|\,\mathcal{A}_{n}\Big]\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;\overset{\eqref{eq:simple:triangle:ineq}}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\eta^{2}\,\Big(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{1}^{2}]}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{2}^{2}]}\Big)^{2}\;,

where

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\Big\|\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}\big(\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{i})\big)-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X^{\prime}_{1})\big)\,\big|\,\theta^{\rm init}\big]\big)\,\Big|\,\mathcal{A}_{n}\Big]\Big\|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big\|\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}\big(\mathbb{E}\big[\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{i})\big)\,\big|\,\theta^{\rm init},X_{i}\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X^{\prime}_{1})\big)\,\big|\,\theta^{\rm init}\big]\big)\Big\|\;,

\displaystyle\Delta_{2}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\Big\|\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}\big(\mathbb{E}\big[\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X^{\prime}_{1})\big)\,\big|\,\theta^{\rm init}\big]-\phi\big(X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_{i}\big)\big)\,\Big|\,\mathcal{A}_{n}\Big]\Big\|

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big\|\,\mathbb{E}\big[\phi\big(K^{m}_{1;\theta^{\rm init}}(X^{\prime}_{1})\big)-\phi\big(X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_{1}\big)\,\big|\,\theta^{\rm init}\big]\Big\|\;,

and
X^{\prime}_{1}
is an i.i.d. copy of
X_{1}
and in particular independent of
\theta^{\rm init}
.
\Delta_{1}
is controlled via (
19
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{1}^{2}]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\epsilon^{2}+(\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{1}^{\nu}])^{2/\nu}\,{\mathbbm{P}}(\Delta_{1}>\epsilon)^{\nu/(\nu-2)}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\epsilon^{2}+\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big\|\phi(K^{m}_{1;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{1}))-\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi(K^{m}_{1;\theta^{\rm init}}(X^{\prime}_{1}))\,\Big|\,\theta^{\rm init}\Big]\Big\|^{\nu}\Big)^{2/\nu}

\displaystyle\hskip 10.00002pt\times{\mathbbm{P}}\bigg(\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\big\| \sum_{i \leq n}
\big(
\mean\big[
\phi\big( K^m_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_i) \big)
\,\big|\, \theta^{\rm init}, X_i \big]
-
\mean\big[ \phi\big( K^m_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X'_1) \big) \,\big|\, \theta^{\rm init} \big]
\big) \big\|}{n}$}}>\epsilon\bigg)^{\frac{\nu-2}{\nu}}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(b)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\epsilon^{2}+\kappa_{\nu;m}^{2}\big(\vartheta^{\rm init}_{n,m}(\epsilon)\big)^{\frac{\nu-2}{\nu}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\big(\varepsilon^{\rm init}_{n,m;\nu}(\epsilon)\big)^{2}\;.

(21)

In
(a)
, we have plugged in the definition of
\Delta_{1}
and applied a Jensen’s inequality with respect to the empirical average; in
(b)
, we have used
A7
to bound the
\nu
-th moment term as

\displaystyle\;\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big\|\phi(K^{m}_{1;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{1}))-\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi(K^{m}_{1;\theta^{\rm init}}(X^{\prime}_{1}))\,\Big|\,\theta^{\rm init}\Big]\Big\|^{\nu}\Big)^{1/\nu}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big(\mathbb{E}\,\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\|\phi(K^{m}_{1;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{1}))-\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi(K^{m}_{1;\theta^{\rm init}}(X^{\prime}_{1}))\,\Big|\,\theta^{\rm init}\Big]\Big\|^{\nu}\,\Big|\,\theta^{\rm init}\Big]\,\Big]\Big)^{1/\nu}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\sup\nolimits_{\psi\in\Psi}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\|\phi(K^{m}_{1;\psi}(X_{1}))-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi(K^{m}_{1;\psi}(X^{\prime}_{1}))\,\big]\Big\|^{\nu}\,\Big]\Big)^{1/\nu}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\sup\nolimits_{\psi\in\Psi}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\|\phi(K^{m}_{1;\psi}(X_{1}))-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi(K^{m}_{1;\psi}(X_{1}))\,\big]\Big\|^{\nu}\,\Big]\Big)^{1/\nu}\;\leq\;\kappa_{\nu;m}

and recalled the definitions of
\vartheta^{\rm init}_{n,m}
and
\varepsilon^{\rm init}_{n,m;\nu}
. On the other hand,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[

\displaystyle\Delta^{2}_{2}]\;=\;\mathbb{E}\Big\|\,\mint_{\mathbbm{R}^{d}}\phi(x)(K^{m}_{1;\theta^{\rm init}}p_{\psi^{*}})(x)dx-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi(X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_{1})\big|\theta^{\rm init}\big]\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big\|\,\mint_{\mathbbm{R}^{d}}(K^{m}_{1;\theta^{\rm init}}\phi)(x)\,p_{\psi^{*}}(x)dx-\mathbb{E}_{p_{\theta^{\rm init}}}[\,\phi\,]\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(b)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big\|\mint_{\mathbbm{R}^{d}}\big(K^{m}_{1;\theta^{\rm init}}\big(\phi-\mathbb{E}_{p_{\theta^{\rm init}}}[\,\phi\,]\big)\big)(x)\times p_{\psi^{*}}(x)dx\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(c)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big\|\mint_{\mathbbm{R}^{d}}\big(K^{m}_{1;\theta^{\rm init}}\big(\phi-\mathbb{E}_{p_{\theta^{\rm init}}}[\,\phi\,]\big)\big)(x)\times(p_{\psi^{*}}(x)-p_{\theta^{\rm init}}(x))dx\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(d)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\msum_{l=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\Big(\mint_{\mathbbm{R}^{d}}\big(K^{m}_{t1;\theta^{\rm init}}\big(\phi-\mathbb{E}_{p_{\theta^{\rm init}}}[\,\phi\,]\big)\big)(x)^{\top}e_{l}\times p_{\theta^{\rm init}}(x)\times\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ p_{\psi^*}(x) - p_{\theta^{\rm init}}(x) }{p_{\theta^{\rm init}(x)}}$}}\,dx\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(e)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\msum_{l=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\mint_{\mathbbm{R}^{d}}\big(\big(K^{m}_{t1;\theta^{\rm init}}\big(\phi-\mathbb{E}_{p_{\theta^{\rm init}}}[\,\phi\,]\big)\big)(x)^{\top}e_{l}\big)^{2}p_{\theta^{\rm init}}(x)dx

\displaystyle\hskip 60.00009pt\times\mint_{\mathbbm{R}^{d}}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ p_{\psi^*}(x) - p_{\theta^{\rm init}}(x) }{p_{\theta^{\rm init}(x)}}$}}\Big)^{2}p_{\theta^{\rm init}}(x)dx\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\;\overset{(f)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\msum_{l=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}\Big(\alpha^{2m}\,\text{\rm Tr}\,\text{\rm Cov}\big[\phi(X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_{1})\,\big|\,\theta^{\rm init}\big]\,\chi^{2}(p_{\psi^{*}},p_{\theta^{\rm init}})\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(g)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\alpha^{2m}\sigma^{2}C_{\chi}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{\rm init}-\psi^{*}\|^{2}\;.

In
(a)
, we have used that
(Kf)(x)=\int K(x,y)f(y)dy
; in
(b)
, we have used that the Markov operator leaves the constant function invariant; in
(c)
, we used that
K^{m}_{1;\theta^{\rm init}}
leaves
p_{\theta^{\rm init}}
invariant; in
(d)
, we denoted
(e_{l})_{l\leq d}
as the standard basis vectors of
\mathbbm{R}^{d}
and multiplied and divided by
p_{\theta^{\rm init}}(x)
; in
(e)
, we have used a Cauchy-Schwarz inequality; in
(f)
, we have used the definition of the spectral gap
\alpha
in
A3
; in
(g)
, we have used
A1
and
A2
. Combining the bounds gives the first inequality that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,\big\|\mathbb{E}\big[\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}-

\displaystyle\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}\,\big|\,\mathcal{A}_{n}\big]\big\|^{2}\;\leq\;\eta^{2}\Big(\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}\,\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{\rm init}-\psi^{*}\|^{2}}\,+\,\varepsilon^{\rm init}_{n,m;\nu}(\epsilon)\Big)^{2}\;.

Now we handle the second quantity by conditioning on
\theta^{\rm init}
and perform a bias-variance decomposition:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,\|\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}\|^{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;\eta^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}\big(\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{i})\big)-\phi(X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_{i})\big)\Big\|^{2}\,\Big|\,\mathcal{A}_{n}\Big]\,\Big]

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\eta^{2}(Q_{B}+Q_{V})\;,

where

\displaystyle Q_{B}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big\|\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}\big(\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{i})\big)-\phi(X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_{i})\big)\,\Big|\,\mathcal{A}_{n}\Big]\Big\|^{2}\;,

\displaystyle Q_{V}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\text{\rm Tr}\Big(\text{\rm Cov}\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}\phi\big(K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{i})\big)\Big|\mathcal{A}_{n}\Big]+\text{\rm Cov}\Big[\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}\phi(X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_{i})\Big|\theta^{\rm init}\Big]\Big)\Big]\;.

Note that the covariance terms separate because
X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_{i}
is independent of
K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{i})
conditioning on
\theta^{\rm init}
.
\eta^{2}Q_{B}
is exactly the quantity controlled above, so it suffices to bound the variance term
Q_{V}
. By explicitly computing the second covariance term while noting that
X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_{1},\ldots,X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_{n}
are conditionally i.i.d. given
\theta^{\rm init}
and
K^{m}_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_{i})
’s are conditionally independent across
1\leq i\leq n
given
\mathcal{A}_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle Q_{V}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sum_{i \leq n} \, \mean[\Tr\, \Cov[ \phi(K^m_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_i)) \,|\, \theta^{\rm init}, X_i ]]}{n^2}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean\big[ \Tr\,\Cov[ \phi( X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_1) | \theta^{\rm init} ] \big]}{n}$}}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean[\Tr\, \Cov[ \phi(K^m_{1;\theta^{\rm init}}(X_1)) \,|\, \theta^{\rm init}, X_1 ]]}{n}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean\big[ \Tr\,\Cov[ \phi( X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_1) | \theta^{\rm init} ] \big]}{n}$}}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\kappa_{\nu;m}^2 + \sigma^2}{n}$}}\;,

where we have used
A4
,
A7
and
A1
in the last line. Combining the bounds, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,\|\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}\|^{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;\eta^{2}(Q_{B}+Q_{V})

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\eta^{2}\Big(\Big(\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}\,\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{\rm init}-\psi^{*}\|^{2}}\,+\,\varepsilon^{\rm init}_{n,m;\nu}(\epsilon)\Big)^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\kappa_{\nu;m}^2 + \sigma^2}{n}$}}\Big)\;.

∎

Lemma E.3
.

Under
A1
,

\mathbb{E}\,\|\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}-\theta^{\rm pop}\|^{2}\,\leq\,\frac{4\eta^{2}\sigma^{2}}{n}\;
.

Proof of
Lemma

E.3
.

Since both
F^{\rm GD}_{\infty}
and
f^{\rm pop}
involve infinite-length Markov chains, the initializations do not matter and we can decouple the stochasticity of
X_{i}
and
K_{i;\theta^{\rm init}}
. In particular,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,\|\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}-\theta^{\rm pop}\|^{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;\eta^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\,\Big\|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}\big(\phi(X_{i})-\phi\big(X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_{i}\big)\big)-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi(X_{1})-\phi\big(X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_{1}\big)\big]\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\eta^{2}\big(Q^{\prime}_{1}+2\sqrt{Q^{\prime}_{1}Q^{\prime}_{2}}+Q^{\prime}_{2}\big)\;,

where

\displaystyle Q^{\prime}_{1}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\,\Big\|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}\big(\phi(X_{i})-\mathbb{E}[\phi(X_{1})]\big)\Big\|^{2}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\Tr\, \Cov[ \phi(X_1) ]}{n}$}}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\Tr\, \nabla^2_{\theta} \log Z(\psi^*)}{n}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma^2}{n}$}}\;,

\displaystyle Q^{\prime}_{2}\;\coloneqq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\,\Big\|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum_{i\leq n}\big(\phi(X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi\big(X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_{1}\big)\big]\big)\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \mean\big[ \Tr\, \Cov[ \phi(X^{\theta^{\rm init}}_1) | \theta^{\rm init} ] \big] }{n}$}}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \mean\big[ \Tr\, \nabla^2_{\theta} \log Z(\theta^{\rm init}) \big] }{n}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma^2}{n}$}}\;.

In the computations above, we have used the relation
\nabla^{2}_{\theta}\log Z(\theta)=\text{\rm Cov}_{X\sim p_{\theta}}[\phi(X)]
and the assumption
\sup_{\theta\in\Psi}\mathrm{tr}(\nabla_{\theta}^{2}\log Z(\theta))=\sigma^{2}
from
A1
. This implies the desired bound.
∎

Lemma E.4
.

Under
A1
,

\mathbb{E}\,\|\theta^{\rm pop}-\psi^{*}\|^{2}\leq\big(1-2\mu\eta+L^{2}\eta^{2}\big)\,\mathbb{E}\,\|\theta^{\rm init}-\psi^{*}\|^{2}\;.

Proof of
Lemma

E.4
.

Recall that

\displaystyle f^{\rm pop}(\theta^{\prime})\;=\;\theta-\eta\,\mathbb{E}\big[\phi(X_{1})-\phi(X^{\theta^{\prime}}_{1})\big]\;=\;\theta-\eta\,\big(\nabla_{\psi}\log Z(\psi^{*})-\nabla_{\psi}\log Z(\theta^{\prime})\big)\;.

By construction,
f^{\rm pop}
is deterministic and
f^{\rm pop}(\psi^{*})=\psi^{*}
. By plugging in the recursions and expanding the square, we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\,\big\|

\displaystyle\theta^{\rm pop}-\psi^{*}\big\|^{2}\;=\;\mathbb{E}\,\big\|f^{\rm pop}(\theta^{\rm init})-f^{\rm pop}(\psi^{*})\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\,\big\|(\theta^{\rm init}-\psi^{*})-\eta(\nabla_{\psi}\log Z(\theta^{\rm init})-\nabla_{\psi}\log Z(\psi^{*}))\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\,\big\|\theta^{\rm init}-\psi^{*}\big\|^{2}-2\eta\,\mathbb{E}\big[\big\langle\theta^{\rm init}-\psi^{*}\,,\,\nabla_{\psi}\log Z(\theta^{\rm init})-\nabla_{\psi}\log Z(\psi^{*})\big\rangle\big]

\displaystyle\;+\eta^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\,\big\|\nabla_{\psi}\log Z(\theta^{\rm init})-\nabla_{\psi}\log Z(\psi^{*})\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\,\big\|\theta^{\rm init}-\psi^{*}\big\|^{2}-2\mu\eta\,\mathbb{E}\,\big\|\theta^{\rm init}-\psi^{*}\big\|^{2}+L^{2}\eta^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\,\big\|\theta^{\rm init}-\psi^{*}\big\|^{2}\;.

In the last line, we have recalled
\inf_{\psi\in\Psi}\lambda_{\min}(\nabla_{\psi}^{2}\log Z(\psi))=\mu
and
\sup_{\theta\in\Psi}\lambda_{\mathrm{\max}}(\nabla_{\psi}^{2}\log Z(\psi))=L
by
A1
and applied
Lemma

C.8
. Combining the coefficients gives the desired statement.
∎

Appendix F
Proofs for offline SGD

We prove
Theorem

B.1
(which directly implies
Theorem

4.3
) and
Theorem

B.2
in this section. The key ingredient of both proofs is
Lemma

F.1
below, which provides an iterative error bound for the SGD-with-replacement scheme by combining different approximation bounds in
Appendix

E
. Throughout this section, we denote
\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\,\big\|\psi^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j}-\psi^{*}\big\|^{2}
.

Lemma F.1
.

Under
A1
,
A2
,
A3
,
A4
and
A7
, we have that for
1\leq j\leq N-1
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j}}\;\leq\;\Big(1-\eta_{t}\Big(\mu-\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{L^2}{2}$}}\eta_{t}\Big)\Big)\,\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}}\,+\,\eta_{t}\Big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{5 \sigma+ 5\kappa_{\nu;m}}{\sqrt{B}}$}}\Big)\;,

where
1-\eta_{t}\big(\mu-\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}-\frac{L^{2}}{2}\eta_{t}\big)>0
.

Proof of
Lemma

F.1
.

We first remark that in view of
Lemma

C.4
, the projection step in
Algorithm

2
does not increase
\delta^{\rm SGD_{w}}_{t,j}
, so it suffices to bound
\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j}
as if projection is not performed on
\psi^{\rm sGDw}_{t,j}
. To apply the results from
Appendix

E
, we identify
\theta^{\rm init}=\psi^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}
and
\eta=\eta_{t}
, which allows us to write
\psi^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j}=\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}
. This also implies
\mathbb{E}\|\theta^{\rm init}-\psi^{*}\|^{2}=\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}
and
\varepsilon^{\rm init}_{n,m;\nu}(\epsilon)\leq\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{\nu;n,m,t}(\epsilon)
. By adding and subtracting the auxiliary gradient updates followed by expanding the square, we obtain

\displaystyle\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j}\;=

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\,\big\|\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}+\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}+\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}-\theta^{\rm pop}+\theta^{\rm pop}-\psi^{*}\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\big\|\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}\big\|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\big\|\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}\big\|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\big\|\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}-\theta^{\rm pop}\big\|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\big\|\theta^{\rm pop}-\psi^{*}\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\;+2\,\mathbb{E}\,\big\langle\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}\,,\,\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}\big\rangle+2\,\mathbb{E}\,\big\langle\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}\,,\,\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}-\theta^{\rm pop}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\;+2\,\mathbb{E}\,\big\langle\mathbb{E}\big[\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}\,\big|\,\theta^{\rm init},\mathcal{F}_{n}\big]\,,\,\theta^{\rm pop}-\psi^{*}\big\rangle+2\,\mathbb{E}\,\big\langle\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}\,,\,\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}-\theta^{\rm pop}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\;+2\,\mathbb{E}\,\big\langle\mathbb{E}\big[\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}-\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}\,\big|\,\mathcal{A}_{n}\big]\,,\,\theta^{\rm pop}-\psi^{*}\big\rangle+2\,\mathbb{E}\,\big\langle\theta^{\rm GD}_{\infty}-\theta^{\rm pop}\,,\,\theta^{\rm pop}-\psi^{*}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\;\overset{(b)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 \eta_t^2 (\sigma^2 + \kappa_{\nu;m}^2)}{\sqrt{B}}$}}\mathbb{I}_{\{B<n\}}+\eta_{t}^{2}\Big(\Big(\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}\,\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}}\,+\,\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)\Big)^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\kappa_{\nu;m}^2 + \sigma^2}{n}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 \eta^2_t \sigma^2}{n}$}}+(1-2\mu\eta_{t}+L^{2}\eta^{2}_{t})\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}

\displaystyle\;+2\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \eta_t \sqrt{\sigma^2 + \kappa_{\nu;m}^2}}{\sqrt{B}}$}}\,\mathbb{I}_{\{B<n\}}\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}\,\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}}\,+\,\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{\kappa_{\nu;m}^2 + \sigma^2}}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;+2\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \eta_t \sqrt{\sigma^2 + \kappa_{\nu;m}^2}}{\sqrt{B}}$}}\,\mathbb{I}_{\{B<n\}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \eta_t \sigma}{\sqrt{n}}$}}+0

\displaystyle\;+2\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}\,\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}}\,+\,\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{\kappa_{\nu;m}^2 + \sigma^2}}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\Big)\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \eta_t \sigma}{\sqrt{n} }$}}

\displaystyle\;+2\eta_{t}\Big(\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}\,\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}}\,+\,\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)\Big)\sqrt{(1-2\mu\eta_{t}+L^{2}\eta^{2}_{t})\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}}

\displaystyle\;+2\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \eta_t \sigma}{\sqrt{n}}$}}\sqrt{(1-2\mu\eta_{t}+L^{2}\eta^{2}_{t})\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}}

\displaystyle\;\overset{(c)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\;\Big(1-2\mu\eta_{t}+L\eta_{t}^{2}+\eta_{t}^{2}\alpha^{2m}\sigma^{2}C^{2}_{\chi}+2\eta_{t}\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}\sqrt{1-2\mu\eta_{t}+L^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}\,\Big)\times\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}

\displaystyle\;+2\bigg(\eta_{t}^{2}\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \eta_t^2 (\sqrt{\sigma^2 + \kappa_{\nu;m}^2} + \sigma) \, \alpha^m \sigma C_\chi}{\sqrt{B}}$}}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt+\eta_{t}\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)\sqrt{1-2\mu\eta_{t}+L_{2}\eta_{t}^{2}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \eta_t \sigma\sqrt{1-2\mu\eta_t + L^2 \eta_t^2} }{\sqrt{n}}$}}\,\bigg)\times\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}}

\displaystyle\;+\eta_{t}^{2}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 (\sigma^2 + \kappa_{\nu;m}^2)}{B}$}}+\big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)\big)^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{5\kappa_{\nu;m}^2 + 9 \sigma^2}{B}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 \sqrt{\sigma^2 + \kappa_{\nu;m}^2} \, \varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)}{\sqrt{B}}$}}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ 8 \sigma\sqrt{\sigma^2 + \kappa_{\nu;m}^2}}{B}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 \sigma\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)}{\sqrt{B}}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;(A_{1})\times\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}+(A_{2})\times\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}}+(A_{3})\;.

In
(a)
, we have expanded the square, used
\mathcal{F}_{n}
defined in
Lemma

E.1
and
\mathcal{A}_{n}
defined in
Lemma

E.2
, and noted that
\theta^{\rm pop}-\psi^{*}
is almost surely constant given
\theta^{\rm init}
; in
(b)
, we have applied Lemmas
E.1
,
E.2
,
E.3
and
E.4
under
A1
,
A2
,
A3
,
A4
and
A7
, and used
\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}
for
a,b\geq 0
; in
(c)
, we have grouped the terms by powers of
\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}
, bounded the indicator function from above by
1
and used
B\leq n
to replace
n
in the denominator by
B
. While the computation above is complicated, we remark that the key steps are taking the conditional expectations for the cross-terms in
(b)
, which gives us a tighter bound than directly applying a triangle inequality of the form
\mathbb{E}\|Y_{1}+Y_{2}\|^{2}\leq(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|Y_{1}\|^{2}}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\|Y_{2}\|^{2}})
. To further simplify the bounds, we seek to bound each coefficient by a square, which yields

\displaystyle(A_{1})\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big(\eta_{t}\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}+\sqrt{1-2\mu\eta_{t}+L\eta_{t}^{2}}\,\Big)^{2}\;,

\displaystyle(A_{2})\;\leq

\displaystyle\;2\Big(\eta_{t}\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}+\sqrt{1-2\mu\eta_{t}+L\eta_{t}^{2}}\,\Big)\times\,\eta_{t}\Big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 \sigma+ 2\kappa_{\nu;m}}{\sqrt{B}}$}}\Big)\;,

\displaystyle(A_{3})\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\eta^{2}_{t}\Big(\big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)\big)^{2}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{8 \sigma^2+ 4\kappa_{\nu;m}^2}{\sqrt{B}}$}}\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{21 \sigma^2 + 17 \kappa_{\nu;m}^2}{B}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\eta_{t}^{2}\Big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{5 \sigma+ 5\kappa_{\nu;m}}{\sqrt{B}}$}}\Big)^{2}\;.

This implies

\displaystyle\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;(A_{1})\times\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}+(A_{2})\times\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}}+(A_{3})

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\Big(\Big(\eta_{t}\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}+\sqrt{1-2\mu\eta_{t}+L\eta_{t}^{2}}\Big)\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}}+\eta_{t}\Big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{5 \sigma+ 5\kappa_{\nu;m}}{\sqrt{B}}$}}\Big)\Big)^{2}\;.

Now note that since
\mu\leq L
by the definitions in
A1
,
2\mu\eta_{t}-L^{2}\eta^{2}_{t}\leq 2L\eta_{t}-L^{2}\eta_{t}^{2}\leq 1
. By using
\sqrt{1-x}\leq 1-\frac{x}{2}
for all
x\leq 1
, we get that

\displaystyle\sqrt{1-2\mu\eta_{t}+L^{2}\eta^{2}_{t}}\;\leq\;1-\mu\eta_{t}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{L^2}{2}$}}\eta^{2}_{t}\;.

Substituting this into the earlier bound and taking a square-root, we obtain the desired bound that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j}}\;\leq\;\Big(1-\eta_{t}\Big(\mu-\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{L^2}{2}$}}\eta_{t}\Big)\Big)\,\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}}\,+\,\eta_{t}\Big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{5 \sigma+ 5\kappa_{\nu;m}}{\sqrt{B}}$}}\Big)\;.

Moreover, since
L\geq\mu
by definition from
A1
, we have

\displaystyle 1-\eta_{t}\Big(\mu-\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{L^2}{2}$}}\eta_{t}\Big)\;=

\displaystyle\;\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{L}{\sqrt{2}}$}}\eta_{t}-1\Big)^{2}+(\sqrt{2}\,L-\mu)\eta_{t}+\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}\eta_{t}\;>\;0\;,

which finishes the proof.
∎

F.1
Proof of Theorem
B.1

Under
A1
,
A2
,
A3
,
A4
and
A7
,
Lemma

F.1
implies

\displaystyle\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j}}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\Big(1-\tilde{\mu}_{m}Ct^{-\beta}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{L^2 C^2}{2}$}}t^{-2\beta}\Big)\,\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j-1}}\,+\,Ct^{-\beta}\,\sigma^{\rm SGDw}_{n,T}

for
1\leq t\leq T
and
1\leq j\leq N
, where we have used
\tilde{\mu}_{m}=\mu-\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}
,
\eta_{t}=Ct^{-\beta}
and

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{5 \sigma+ 5\kappa_{\nu;m}}{\sqrt{B}}$}}\;\leq\;\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,T;\nu}(\epsilon)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{5 \sigma+ 5\kappa_{\nu;m}}{\sqrt{B}}$}}\;=\;\sigma^{\rm SGDw}_{n,T}\;.

By an induction on
j=1,\ldots,N
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,N}}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\Big(1-\tilde{\mu}_{m}Ct^{-\beta}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{L^2 C^2}{2}$}}t^{-2\beta}\Big)^{N}\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,0}}

\displaystyle\;\;\,+\,C\sigma^{\rm SGDw}_{n,T}\,t^{-\beta}\,\msum_{j=1}^{N}\Big(1-\tilde{\mu}_{m}Ct^{-\beta}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{L^2 C^2}{2}$}}t^{-2\beta}\Big)^{j-1}\;.

By noting that
\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,0}=\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t-1,N}
almost surely for
t\geq 2
and using another induction on
t=1,\ldots,T
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{T,N}}\,\leq

\displaystyle\;Q_{0}\,\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{0,0}}\,+\,C\sigma^{\rm SGDw}_{n,T}\msum_{t=1}^{T}Q_{t}\,A_{t}\,t^{-\beta}\;,

(22)

where

\displaystyle Q_{t}\coloneqq\mprod_{s=t+1}^{T}\Big(1-\tilde{\mu}_{m}Cs^{-\beta}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{L^2 C^2}{2}$}}s^{-2\beta}\Big)^{N}\;\text{ and }\;A_{t}\coloneqq\msum_{j=1}^{N}\Big(1-\tilde{\mu}_{m}Ct^{-\beta}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{L^2 C^2}{2}$}}t^{-2\beta}\Big)^{j-1}\;.

Note that by
Lemma

F.1
, we also have that
1-\tilde{\mu}_{m}Ct^{-\beta}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{L^2 C^2}{2}$}}t^{-2\beta}>0
.
This allows us to apply
Lemma

C.3
to obtain

\displaystyle\kappa_{0}\;\leq\;\exp\Big(1-N\tilde{\mu}_{m}C\varphi_{1-\beta}(T+1)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ N L^2 C^2}{2}$}}\varphi_{1-2\beta}(T+1)\Big)\;=\;E_{1}^{T,N}\;.

To control
\sum_{t=1}^{T}Q_{t}A_{t}t^{-\beta}
, recall that
\beta\in[0,1]
,
\tilde{\mu}_{m}>0
,
\frac{L^{2}C^{2}}{2}>0
, and that

\displaystyle E_{2}^{T,N}\;=

\displaystyle\;\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{N \tilde\mu_m C}{2}$}}\varphi_{1-\beta}(T+1)+2NL^{2}C^{2}\varphi_{1-2\beta}(T+1)\Big)\;.

We can now apply
Lemma

C.3
: If
\beta\not\in\{\frac{1}{2},1\}
, then

\displaystyle\msum_{t=1}^{T}Q_{t}\,A_{t}\,t^{-\beta}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2^{2\beta+1}}{\tilde\mu_m C}$}}e^{\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{m}C}{2(1-\beta)}\frac{N}{(T+1)^{\beta}}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3^\beta(1+\tilde\mu_m C)^{N-1} (T+2)^{\beta} }{L^2C^2}$}}\,E_{2}^{T,N}\;.

If
\beta=\frac{1}{2}
, i.e.
2\beta=1
, we have

\displaystyle\msum_{t=1}^{T}Q_{t}\,A_{t}\,t^{-\beta}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4}{\tilde\mu_m C}$}}e^{\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{m}CN}{(T+1)^{1/2}}}+2N(1+\tilde{\mu}_{m}C)^{N-1}\varphi_{\frac{1}{2}-L^{2}C^{2}N}(T+1)\,E_{2}^{T,N}\;.

If
\beta=1
, we get that

\displaystyle\msum_{t=1}^{T}Q_{t}\,A_{t}\,t^{-\beta}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4}{\tilde\mu_m C}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3 N \big(1+\frac{L^2C^2}{2}\big)^{N-1} \, e^{2 L^2 C^2 N} \, \log(T+1)}{(T+1)^{(\tilde\mu_m C N)/2}}$}}

Substituting the bounds into (
22
), we get the desired bound that
\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{T,N}}
is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\begin{cases}E_{1}^{T,N}\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{0,0}}\,+\,C\sigma^{\rm SGDw}_{n,T}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4 e^{\frac{\tilde\mu_m C N}{(T+1)^{1/2}}}}{\tilde\mu_m C}$}}+2N(1+\tilde{\mu}_{m}C)^{N-1}\varphi_{\frac{1}{2}-L^{2}C^{2}N}(T+1)\,E_{2}^{T,N}\Big)\\
\hskip 344.00046pt\text{ for }\beta=\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{2}$}}\;,\\[8.00003pt]
E_{1}^{T,N}\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{0,0}}+C\sigma^{\rm SGDw}_{n,T}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{4}{\tilde\mu_m C}$}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3 N \big(1+\frac{L^2C^2}{2}\big)^{N-1} \, e^{2 L^2 C^2 N} \, \log(T+1)}{(T+1)^{(\tilde\mu_m C N)/2}}$}}\Big)\hskip 40.00006pt\text{ for }\beta=1\;,\\[8.00003pt]
E_{1}^{T,N}\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{0,0}}\,+\,C\sigma^{\rm SGDw}_{n,T}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2^{2\beta+1}}{\tilde\mu_m C}$}}e^{\frac{\tilde{\mu}_{m}C}{2(1-\beta)}\frac{N}{(T+1)^{\beta}}}+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{3^\beta(1+\tilde\mu_m C)^{N-1} (T+2)^{\beta} }{L^2C^2}$}}\,E_{2}^{T,N}\Big)\hskip 1.99997pt\text{ otherwise}\;.\end{cases}

∎

F.2
Proof of Theorem
B.2

Recall that
\delta^{\rm SGDo}_{t,j}\coloneqq\mathbb{E}\,\big\|\psi^{\rm SGDo}_{t,j}-\psi^{*}\big\|^{2}
.
To apply the results from
Appendix

E
to
\psi^{\rm SGDo}_{t,j}
, we condition on
S^{o}_{t}
, which in particular fixes
S^{o}_{t,j}
, the last size-
B
subset of
[n]
chosen. We then identify
\theta^{\rm init}=\psi^{\rm SGDo}_{t,j-1}
,
\eta=\eta_{t}
and the dataset used as
\mathcal{D}^{o}_{t,j}\coloneqq(X_{i}\,:\,i\in S^{o}_{t,j})
, which allows us to identify
\psi^{\rm SGDo}_{t,j}=\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}
, the full-batch gradient descent update using
\mathcal{D}^{o}_{t,j}
. Meanwhile, we note that almost surely
\theta^{\rm GD}_{m}=\theta^{\rm SGDw}_{m,B}
, the SGD-with-replacement iterate that uses the full dataset
\mathcal{D}^{o}_{t,j}
. Observe that the proof of
Lemma

F.1
holds with
\delta^{\rm SGDo}_{t,j-1}
replaced by any random initialization
\theta^{\rm init}
possibly correlated with
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
, which allows us to obtain

\displaystyle\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDo}_{t,j}}\;\leq\;\Big(1-\eta_{t}\Big(\mu-\alpha^{m}\sigma C_{\chi}-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{L^2}{2}$}}\eta_{t}\Big)\Big)\,\sqrt{\delta^{\rm SGDo}_{t,j-1}}\,+\,\eta_{t}\Big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,t;\nu}(\epsilon)+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{5 \sigma+ 5\kappa_{\nu;m}}{\sqrt{B}}$}}\Big)\;.

Since the error recursion for
\delta^{\rm SGDo}_{t,j}
is identical to that of
\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{t,j}
in
Lemma

F.1
, the proof of
Theorem

4.3
follows directly, thereby yielding an identical result for
\delta^{\rm SGDo}_{T,N}
as with
\delta^{\rm SGDw}_{T,N}
in
Theorem

4.3
.
∎

Appendix G
Proofs for tail probability bounds in offline SGD

We present the proofs for results in
Section

B.3
that control the tail probability terms
\vartheta^{\rm SGDw}_{\nu;n,m,T}
and
\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{\nu;n,m,T}
.

Proof of
Lemma

B.3
.

For
\delta>0
, let
N_{\delta}
be the
\delta
-covering number of
\Psi
, which satisfies
N_{\delta}\leq\big(r_{\Psi}(1+2/\delta)\big)^{p}
(Example 5.8,
[
51
]
). Note also that by the Jensen’s inequality applied to
\mathbb{E}[{\,\vbox{\hbox{\tiny$\bullet$}}\,}|X_{1}]
and Assumption
A6
, there exist some
\sigma_{m},\zeta_{m}>0
such that, for any
z\in\mathbbm{R}^{p}
with
\|z\|\leq\zeta_{m}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[e^{z^{\top}(\mathbb{E}[\phi(K^{m}_{\psi^{*}}(X_{1}))|X_{1}]-\mathbb{E}[\phi(K^{m}_{\psi^{*}}(X_{1}))])}]\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}[e^{z^{\top}(\phi(K^{m}_{\psi^{*}}(X_{1}))-\mathbb{E}[\phi(K^{m}_{\psi^{*}}(X_{1}))])}]\;\leq\;e^{\sigma_{m}^{2}\|z\|^{2}/2}\;.

Meanwhile under
A5
, recycling the proof of Lemma 3.1 of
[
21
]
shows that if
C_{m}\delta/\sqrt{p}<\sigma^{2}_{m}\zeta_{m}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{P}}\Big(\sup_{\psi\in\Psi}\Big\|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\phi\big(K^{m}_{\psi}(X_{i})\big)\big|X_{i}\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi(K^{m}_{\psi}(

\displaystyle X_{i}))\big]\Big\|>3C_{m}\delta\Big)\;\leq\;2N_{\delta}p\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n C^2_m \delta^2}{2 p \sigma_m^2}$}}\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;2(r_{\Psi})^{p}\exp\Big(p\log(1+2\delta^{-1})-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n C^2_m \delta^2}{2 p \sigma_m^2}$}}\Big)\;.

Since the probability above is an upper bound to
\vartheta^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,T}(3C_{m}\delta)
, we get that if
C_{m}\delta/\sqrt{p}<\sigma^{2}_{m}\zeta_{m}
,

\displaystyle\Big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{\nu;n,m,T}(3

\displaystyle C_{m}\delta)\Big)^{2}\;=\;9C_{m}^{2}\delta^{2}+\kappa_{\nu;m}^{2}\Big(\vartheta^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,T}\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{C_m \delta}{\sqrt{p}}$}}\Big)\Big)^{\frac{\nu-2}{\nu}}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;9C_{m}^{2}\delta^{2}+\kappa_{\nu;m}^{2}2^{\frac{2(\nu-2)}{\nu}}(r_{\Psi})^{\frac{(\nu-2)p}{\nu}}\exp\Big(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(\nu-2)p}{\nu}$}}\log(1+2\delta^{-1})-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{n (\nu-2) C^2_m \delta^2}{2 \nu p \sigma_m^2}$}}\Big)\;.

Recall that by assumption,
\frac{\log n}{n}<\frac{\sigma^{2}_{m}\zeta^{2}_{m}}{p+\nu-2}
. We now choose

\displaystyle\delta\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_m}{C_m}$}}\sqrt{p\Big(p+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 \nu}{\nu-2}$}}\Big)\times\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\log n}{n}$}}}\;=\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sigma_m}{C_m}$}}\sqrt{p\times\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(\nu- 2) p + 2 \nu}{\nu-2}$}}\times\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\log n}{n}$}}}\;,

which implies

\displaystyle\;\inf_{\epsilon>0}\Big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{\nu;n,m,T}(\epsilon)\Big)^{2}\;\leq\;\Big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{\nu;n,m,T}(3C_{m}\delta)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{9\sigma_m^2 p ( (\nu-2)p + 2 \nu) \log n }{(\nu- 2) n}$}}\,+\,\kappa_{\nu;m}^{2}2^{\frac{\nu-2}{\nu}}(r_{\Psi})^{\frac{(\nu-2)p}{\nu}}(1+2\delta^{-1})^{\frac{(\nu-2)p}{\nu}}\exp\Big(-\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{(\nu-2) p + 2 \nu}{2 \nu}$}}\log n\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{9\sigma_m^2 p ( (\nu-2)p +2 \nu) \log n }{(\nu- 2) n}$}}

\displaystyle\qquad\,+\,\kappa_{\nu;m}^{2}2^{\frac{\nu-2}{\nu}}(r_{\Psi})^{\frac{(\nu-2)p}{\nu}}\Big(1+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 C_m (\nu-2)^{1/2}}{\sigma_m p^{1/2} ((\nu-2)p + 2\nu)^{1/2}}$}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{\log n}}$}}\Big)^{\frac{(\nu-2)p}{\nu}}n^{-\frac{(\nu-2)p+2\nu}{2\nu}}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{9\sigma_m^2 p ( (\nu-2)p +2 \nu) \log n }{(\nu- 2) n}$}}

\displaystyle\qquad\,+\,\kappa_{\nu;m}^{2}2^{\frac{\nu-2}{\nu}}(r_{\Psi})^{\frac{(\nu-2)p}{\nu}}\Big(1+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 C_m (\nu-2)^{1/2}}{\sigma_m p^{1/2} ((\nu-2)p + 2\nu)^{1/2}}$}}\Big)^{\frac{(\nu-2)p}{\nu}}n^{\frac{(\nu-2)p}{2\nu}-\frac{(\nu-2)p+2\nu}{2\nu}}

\displaystyle\;\leq\;\bigg(\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{9\sigma_m^2 p ( (\nu-2)p +2 \nu) }{\nu- 2}$}}+\kappa_{\nu;m}^{2}2^{\frac{(\nu-2)}{\nu}}(r_{\Psi})^{\frac{(\nu-2)p}{\nu}}\Big(1+\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{2 C_m (\nu-2)^{1/2}}{\sigma_m p^{1/2} ((\nu-2)p + 2\nu)^{1/2}}$}}\Big)^{\frac{(\nu-2)p}{\nu}}\bigg)\,\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\log n}{n}$}}\;.

Taking a squareroot across and using
\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}
for
a,b>0
gives the desired bound. The limiting result follows by substituting this bound into
Theorem

B.1
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

B.4
.

Let
\delta>0
and
N_{\delta}
be defined as in the proof of
Lemma

B.3
, with
N_{\delta}\leq\big(r_{\Psi}(1+2/\delta)\big)^{p}
. Let
(\psi_{l})_{l=1}^{N_{\delta}}
be the centers of the covering
\delta
-balls. The covering-ball argument of the proof of Lemma 3.1 of
[
21
]
shows that

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,T}(3C_{m}\delta)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;{\mathbbm{P}}\Big(\,\sup_{\psi\in\Psi}\Big\|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\phi\big(K^{m}_{\psi}(X_{i})\big)\big|X_{i}\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi(K^{m}_{\psi}(X_{i}))\big]\Big\|>3C_{m}\delta\Big)

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{l=1}^{N_{\delta}}{\mathbbm{P}}\Big(\Big\|\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{1}{n}$}}\msum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[\phi\big(K^{m}_{\psi_{l}}(X_{i})\big)\big|X_{i}\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\phi(K^{m}_{\psi_{l}}(X_{i}))\big]\Big\|\geq C_{m}\delta\Big)\;.

By a Markov’s inequality followed by the Burkholder’s inequality applied to an average of i.i.d. summands (see e.g.
[
52
]
for
\nu>2
), there exists a constant
C_{\nu}>0
depending only on
\nu
such that

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,T}(3C_{m}\delta)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{l=1}^{N_{\delta}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean\Big\| \msum_{i=1}^n \mean\big[ \phi\big(K^m_{\psi_l}(X_i) \big) \big| X_i \big]
-
\mean\big[ \phi(K^m_{\psi_l}( X_i))
\big] \Big\|^\nu}{n^\nu C_m^\nu\delta^\nu}$}}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\msum_{l=1}^{N_{\delta}}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\mean\big\| \mean\big[ \phi\big(K^m_{\psi_l}(X_1) \big) \big| X_i \big]
-
\mean\big[ \phi(K^m_{\psi_l}( X_1))
\big] \big\|^\nu}{n^{\nu/2} C_m^\nu\delta^\nu}$}}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{N_\delta\kappa_{\nu;m}^\nu}{n^{\nu/2} C_m^\nu\delta^\nu}$}}\;\leq\;\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{( r_\Psi)^p (1+2\delta^{-1})^p \kappa_{\nu;m}^\nu}{n^{\nu/2} C_m^\nu\delta^\nu}$}}\;,

where we have used
A4
in the last line. This implies

\displaystyle\Big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{\nu;n,m,T}(3C_{m}\delta)\Big)^{2}\;=

\displaystyle\;9C_{m}^{2}\delta^{2}+\kappa_{\nu;m}^{2}\Big(\vartheta^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,T}(3C_{m}\delta)\Big)^{(\nu-2)/\nu}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;9C_{m}^{2}\delta^{2}+\kappa_{\nu;m}^{\nu}(r_{\Psi})^{\frac{(\nu-2)p}{\nu}}C_{m}^{-(\nu-2)}\times\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ (1+2\delta^{-1})^{\frac{(\nu-2) p}{\nu}}}{n^{\frac{\nu-2}{2}} \delta^{\nu-2}}$}}\;.

Choosing
\delta=n^{-\frac{(\nu-2)\nu}{2(\nu^{2}+(\nu-2)p)}}\leq 1
, we get that

\displaystyle\inf_{\epsilon>0}\Big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{\nu;n,m,T}

\displaystyle\,(\epsilon)\Big)^{2}\;\leq\;\Big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{\nu;n,m,T}(3C_{m}\delta)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;9C_{m}^{2}n^{-\frac{(\nu-2)\nu}{\nu^{2}+(\nu-2)p}}+\kappa_{\nu;m}^{\nu}(r_{\Psi})^{\frac{(\nu-2)p}{\nu}}C_{m}^{-(\nu-2)}3^{\frac{(\nu-2)p}{\nu}}\,n^{-\frac{(\nu-2)\nu}{\nu^{2}+(\nu-2)p}}

\displaystyle\;=

\displaystyle\;\big(9C_{m}^{2}+\kappa_{\nu;m}^{\nu}(r_{\Psi})^{\frac{(\nu-2)p}{\nu}}C_{m}^{-(\nu-2)}3^{\frac{(\nu-2)p}{\nu}}\big)\,n^{-\frac{(\nu-2)\nu}{\nu^{2}+(\nu-2)p}}\;.

Taking a squareroot across and using
\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}
for
a,b>0
gives the desired bound. The limiting result follows by substituting this bound into
Theorem

B.1
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

B.5
.

By a Markov’s inequality and a Jensen’s inequality with respect to the empirical average, we have

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,T}(\epsilon)\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\sup_{t\in[T],j\in[N]}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{ \sum_{i=1}^n \mean\Big\| \mean\Big[ \phi\Big(K^m_{\psi^{\rm SGDw}_{t-1, j}}(X_i) \Big) \Big| X_i, \psi^{\rm SGDw}_{t-1,j} \Big]
-
\mean\Big[ \phi\Big(K^m_{\psi^{\rm SGDw}_{t-1,j}}(X'_1)\Big) \Big| \psi^{\rm SGDw}_{t-1,j}
\Big] \Big\| }{n \epsilon}$}}

\displaystyle\;\eqqcolon

\displaystyle\;\sup_{t\in[T],j\in[N]}\mathord{\raise 0.49991pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\genfrac{}{}{0.4pt}{}{\sum_{i=1}^n A_{tji}}{n \epsilon}$}}\;.

Meanwhile by a triangle inequality and the ergodicity assumption, we have

\displaystyle A_{tji}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\mathbb{E}\Big\|\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi\Big(K^{m}_{\psi^{\rm SGDw}_{t-1,j}}(X_{i})\Big)\Big|X_{i},\psi^{\rm SGDw}_{t-1,j}\Big]-\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi\Big(X^{\psi^{\rm SGDw}_{t-1,j}}_{1}\Big)\Big|\psi^{\rm SGDw}_{t-1,j}\Big]\Big\|

\displaystyle\;+\mathbb{E}\Big\|\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi\Big(X^{\psi^{\rm SGDw}_{t-1,j}}_{1}\Big)\Big|\psi^{\rm SGDw}_{t-1,j}\Big]-\mathbb{E}\Big[\phi\Big(K^{m}_{\psi^{\rm SGDw}_{t-1,j}}(X^{\prime}_{1})\Big)\Big|\psi^{\rm SGDw}_{t-1,j}\Big]\Big\|

\displaystyle\;\leq

\displaystyle\;2\tilde{C}_{K}\tilde{\alpha}^{m}\;.

This implies
\vartheta^{\rm SGDw}_{n,m,T}(\epsilon)\leq 2\tilde{C}_{K}\tilde{\alpha}^{m}\epsilon^{-1}
, and therefore

\displaystyle\big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{\nu;n,m,T}(\epsilon)\big)^{2}\;\leq\;\epsilon^{2}+2^{\frac{\nu-2}{\nu}}\kappa_{\nu;m}^{2}(\tilde{C}_{K})^{\frac{\nu-2}{\nu}}\tilde{\alpha}^{\frac{(\nu-2)m}{\nu}}\epsilon^{-\frac{\nu-2}{\nu}}\;.

Choosing
\epsilon=\tilde{\alpha}^{(\nu-2)m/(3\nu-2)}
gives

\displaystyle\inf\nolimits_{\epsilon>0}\big(\varepsilon^{\rm SGDw}_{\nu;n,m,T}(\epsilon)\big)^{2}\;\leq

\displaystyle\;\big(1+2^{\frac{\nu-2}{\nu}}\kappa_{\nu;m}^{2}(\tilde{C}_{K})^{\frac{\nu-2}{\nu}}\big)\tilde{\alpha}^{\frac{2(\nu-2)m}{3\nu-2}}\;.

Taking a squareroot across and using
\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}
for
a,b>0
gives the desired bound. The limiting result follows by substituting this bound into
Theorem

B.1
.
∎

NeurIPS Paper Checklist

1.

Claims

Question: Do the main claims made in the abstract and introduction accurately reflect the paper’s contributions and scope?

Answer:
[Yes]

Justification: Our abstract summarizes the theoretical results of our paper. We cleary mention that while CD
can
achieve these rates,
assumptions are needed to guarantee this. We also mention the model class studied in this paper, namely (unnormalized) exponential family distributions.

Guidelines:

•

The answer NA means that the abstract and introduction do not include the claims made in the paper.

•

The abstract and/or introduction should clearly state the claims made, including the contributions made in the paper and important assumptions and limitations. A No or NA answer to this question will not be perceived well by the reviewers.

•

The claims made should match theoretical and experimental results, and reflect how much the results can be expected to generalize to other settings.

•

It is fine to include aspirational goals as motivation as long as it is clear that these goals are not attained by the paper.

2.

Limitations

Question: Does the paper discuss the limitations of the work performed by the authors?

Answer:
[Yes]

Justification: We mention limitations multiple times in the main body of our work: after introducing the assumptions in Section
3.1
,
we mention that spectral gaps may not be verified for heavy tail distributions, or act numerically unfavorably for multimodal distributions,
and that constants may be large in practice.
In the discussion (Section
6
), we reflect back on these limitations, and we mention the limitations of our considered model
class (unnormalized exponential families).

Guidelines:

•

The answer NA means that the paper has no limitation while the answer No means that the paper has limitations, but those are not discussed in the paper.

•

The authors are encouraged to create a separate "Limitations" section in their paper.

•

The paper should point out any strong assumptions and how robust the results are to violations of these assumptions (e.g., independence assumptions, noiseless settings, model well-specification, asymptotic approximations only holding locally). The authors should reflect on how these assumptions might be violated in practice and what the implications would be.

•

The authors should reflect on the scope of the claims made, e.g., if the approach was only tested on a few datasets or with a few runs. In general, empirical results often depend on implicit assumptions, which should be articulated.

•

The authors should reflect on the factors that influence the performance of the approach. For example, a facial recognition algorithm may perform poorly when image resolution is low or images are taken in low lighting. Or a speech-to-text system might not be used reliably to provide closed captions for online lectures because it fails to handle technical jargon.

•

The authors should discuss the computational efficiency of the proposed algorithms and how they scale with dataset size.

•

If applicable, the authors should discuss possible limitations of their approach to address problems of privacy and fairness.

•

While the authors might fear that complete honesty about limitations might be used by reviewers as grounds for rejection, a worse outcome might be that reviewers discover limitations that aren’t acknowledged in the paper. The authors should use their best judgment and recognize that individual actions in favor of transparency play an important role in developing norms that preserve the integrity of the community. Reviewers will be specifically instructed to not penalize honesty concerning limitations.

3.

Theory Assumptions and Proofs

Question: For each theoretical result, does the paper provide the full set of assumptions and a complete (and correct) proof?

Answer:
[Yes]

Justification: All of our theoretical results are formally proved in the supplementary material.
In the main paper, we provide some proof sketches (discussion of the tail term in Section
4
,
paragraphs after theorems in section
3
) to provide high level intuitions behind the proofs.
All of the theoretical results provided by our work are proved in the supplementary material,
usually by invoking (and referencing) more atomic lemmas, also proved in the supplementary material.
All of our theorems and lemmas are stated with the assumptions they require.

Guidelines:

•

The answer NA means that the paper does not include theoretical results.

•

All the theorems, formulas, and proofs in the paper should be numbered and cross-referenced.

•

All assumptions should be clearly stated or referenced in the statement of any theorems.

•

The proofs can either appear in the main paper or the supplemental material, but if they appear in the supplemental material, the authors are encouraged to provide a short proof sketch to provide intuition.

•

Inversely, any informal proof provided in the core of the paper should be complemented by formal proofs provided in appendix or supplemental material.

•

Theorems and Lemmas that the proof relies upon should be properly referenced.

4.

Experimental Result Reproducibility

Question: Does the paper fully disclose all the information needed to reproduce the main experimental results of the paper to the extent that it affects the main claims and/or conclusions of the paper (regardless of whether the code and data are provided or not)?

Answer:
[N/A]

Justification: The paper does not include experiments.

Guidelines:

•

The answer NA means that the paper does not include experiments.

•

If the paper includes experiments, a No answer to this question will not be perceived well by the reviewers: Making the paper reproducible is important, regardless of whether the code and data are provided or not.

•

If the contribution is a dataset and/or model, the authors should describe the steps taken to make their results reproducible or verifiable.

•

Depending on the contribution, reproducibility can be accomplished in various ways. For example, if the contribution is a novel architecture, describing the architecture fully might suffice, or if the contribution is a specific model and empirical evaluation, it may be necessary to either make it possible for others to replicate the model with the same dataset, or provide access to the model. In general. releasing code and data is often one good way to accomplish this, but reproducibility can also be provided via detailed instructions for how to replicate the results, access to a hosted model (e.g., in the case of a large language model), releasing of a model checkpoint, or other means that are appropriate to the research performed.

•

While NeurIPS does not require releasing code, the conference does require all submissions to provide some reasonable avenue for reproducibility, which may depend on the nature of the contribution. For example

(a)

If the contribution is primarily a new algorithm, the paper should make it clear how to reproduce that algorithm.

(b)

If the contribution is primarily a new model architecture, the paper should describe the architecture clearly and fully.

(c)

If the contribution is a new model (e.g., a large language model), then there should either be a way to access this model for reproducing the results or a way to reproduce the model (e.g., with an open-source dataset or instructions for how to construct the dataset).

(d)

We recognize that reproducibility may be tricky in some cases, in which case authors are welcome to describe the particular way they provide for reproducibility. In the case of closed-source models, it may be that access to the model is limited in some way (e.g., to registered users), but it should be possible for other researchers to have some path to reproducing or verifying the results.

5.

Open access to data and code

Question: Does the paper provide open access to the data and code, with sufficient instructions to faithfully reproduce the main experimental results, as described in supplemental material?

Answer:
[N/A]

Justification: The paper does not include experiments, and thus no code or data.

Guidelines:

•

The answer NA means that paper does not include experiments requiring code.

•

Please see the NeurIPS code and data submission guidelines (
https://nips.cc/public/guides/CodeSubmissionPolicy
) for more details.

•

While we encourage the release of code and data, we understand that this might not be possible, so “No” is an acceptable answer. Papers cannot be rejected simply for not including code, unless this is central to the contribution (e.g., for a new open-source benchmark).

•
